# Dual-GPU (tp=2, P2P) — change manifest

> **Goal.** Run the Qwen3.8-27B NVFP4 checkpoint on two RTX 5060 Ti (16 GB each, sm_120) with tensor-parallel tp=2 over P2P, no new dependencies (no NCCL), preserving the single-active-request DSpark losslessness property and the existing serving surface.
>
> **Hardware.** 2x NVIDIA RTX 5060 Ti · sm_120 · 16 GB each · PCIe (no NVLink on these cards); P2P availability UNVERIFIED -- see CHG-0027
> **Weights.** Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-RTX5090: 17.9 GB -- does NOT fit one 16 GB card; tp=2 is a memory necessity, not an optimization
>
> **Plan.** [01-identification-plan.md](01-identification-plan.md) · **Consumed by** 03-implementation-plan.md (next step): only items with status planned/implemented/verified flow into the final plan's PR decomposition
>
> **Status.** last scan: {'new_items': 1015, 'total_items': 3115} · 3115 items · created 2026-09-25

## How to read this file

| field | meaning |
|---|---|
| `id` | `CHG-####`, stable, never reused |
| status `candidate` | recorded site or hypothesis, not yet audited against code |
| status `confirmed` | audited; the change is real and required for tp=2 |
| status `planned` | assigned to the final implementation plan (next step sets this) |
| status `implemented` | code merged |
| status `verified` | validated per the item's verification field |
| status `rejected` | audited and no change needed (note MUST say why) |

A `change` of `null` means *the required change has not yet been designed* — that is exactly what the next step (the final implementation plan) is for. Items with `change: null` and `status: confirmed` are the raw material.

### device-init — Device initialization & context model

**Paths:** `runtime/src/runtime.cpp`, `runtime/include/sparkinfer/runtime.h`, `server/src/model_engine.cpp`, `runtime/include/sparkinfer/device_health.h`
**Why.** The whole process today establishes exactly one 'current device' (thread-local cudaSetDevice in RuntimeImpl::initialize) and every kernel/stream/graph inherits it. The dual-GPU design decision (stream-scoped launches vs per-context setDevice; how many CUDA contexts coexist in one process) is made here.

- [ ] RuntimeConfig.device_id (single int) -> device list + tp size
- [ ] ModelEngine::load cudaGetDeviceCount gate (currently >=1) -> >= tp, and the requested ids must exist
- [ ] Runtime property queries (SM count, mem clock, bus width, PCI bus id) per device
- [ ] device_health: note_cuda_error/device_lost are process-global; decide fail-fast (recommended: process death on any GPU error, matching current ops) vs isolate
- [ ] Confirm CUDA 'current device' is thread-local and document which threads may touch which device (server worker thread, HTTP threads doing submit-time memcpy, lmcache sidecar process)

### kv-cache — Paged KV cache

**Paths:** `runtime/src/kv_cache.cpp`, `runtime/include/sparkinfer/kv_cache.h`, `runtime/csrc/cuda/kv_ops.cu`
**Why.** One flat pool per of {K,V} (plus int8 scale pools, windowed ring tables, per-sequence block tables) on a single device. KV heads must be split across the two devices; everything built on top (paging, prefix sharing, admission, metrics, lmcache) reads those pools.

- [ ] k_pool/v_pool layout [slots, blocks, block_size, kv_heads, head_dim] -> per-device pool holding kv_heads/2 each (or by slot, if head split is uneven)
- [ ] Block tables: one per device pool; logical block numbering shared, physical independent
- [ ] int8 KV scale pools (k_scale/v_scale) follow the same split
- [ ] Windowed (SWA) ring slots and d_win_tables per pool
- [ ] Prefix sharing refcounts are per physical pool (blocks are never shared across devices; a logical prefix = two halves)
- [ ] Admission: KV capacity = min over pools (a session needs blocks in every pool it touches)
- [ ] Auto-sizing budget (80% free VRAM in examples; server formula in ModelEngine) becomes a per-device budget
- [ ] free_kv_blocks / prefix-cache metrics per pool

### gdn-state — Gated-DeltaNet recurrent state

**Paths:** `runtime/src/models/qwen35.cpp`, `runtime/src/models/qwen35_prefill.cpp`
**Why.** 48 of 64 Qwen3.8 layers are GDN and carry per-sequence recurrent state (fp32 lin_state + bf16 conv state) allocated at open_session on one device. The state is per value-head, so it splits cleanly across GPUs -- but every snapshot/restore/rollback path must follow.

- [ ] lin_state / lin_conv_state allocation in open_session: split by linear_v_heads (24/24 with 48 heads) or by block convention (gdn_qh_block)
- [ ] All snapshot paths: prefix-cache pinned-host snapshots (~205 MB on 27B), dflash spec save/restore snapshots, packed-decode bf16 compaction
- [ ] Prefill recurrent-state initialization (conv state zeroing) per device
- [ ] Verify no cross-head coupling in the GDN update (it must be independent per v-head or the split is invalid)

### moe — MoE router & expert dispatch

**Paths:** `moe/src/engine.cpp`, `moe/src/router.cpp`, `moe/include/sparkinfer/moe/`
**Why.** MoEEngine allocates its scratch (router logits, top-k ids, weights, counts) on the current device and all expert weights live on that device. Qwen3.8-27B is dense_ffn (n_experts=1, top_k=1) so it takes the dense special-case; Qwen3.6-35B-A3B would split 256 experts.

- [ ] Scratch buffers per MoEEngine instance (one per device)
- [ ] 27B: single 'expert' = one big SwiGLU FFN -> column-split gate/up, row-split down (see layout appendix)
- [ ] 35B-A3B: expert residency split (E/2 per device) incl. expert_cache_slots budget and async_expert_prefetch per device
- [ ] Router GEMM: input hidden is full on both devices -> replicate the router or run on device 0 and broadcast; counts/dispatch lists are then per device
- [ ] launch_moe_expert_ffn* kernels: dispatch only this device's experts

### weight-loading — Weight loading & conversion

**Paths:** `runtime/src/gguf.cpp`, `runtime/src/safetensors.cpp`, `runtime/src/models/qwen35.cpp (load_gguf/load_compressed_tensors/load_weights)`, `runtime/src/models/qwen_vision.cpp`, `runtime/src/models/dflash_draft.cpp`, `runtime/tools/`
**Why.** Every loader mallocs the complete model into one device (17.9 GB > 16 GB). Loaders must place each tensor per the ownership map, including the opportunistic extra-format copies (o_fp4/down_fp4/wo_fp4/gate_fp4) that are currently gated on one device's free VRAM.

- [ ] Per-tensor ownership map (P3 deliverable) consumed by all three loaders (GGUF, compressed-tensors, flat .bin)
- [ ] GGUF quantized-in-VRAM experts: split by expert index; dequant scratch per device
- [ ] Free-VRAM preflight gates become per-device decisions (an fp4 copy may fit on one card and not the other)
- [ ] DSpark drafter placement (replicate vs split) per CHG-0030
- [ ] Vision tower placement per CHG-0031
- [ ] runtime/tools/convert_qwen35.py: output layout must support split placement (or prove it doesn't need to)

### decode-path — Decode hot path (single/packed token)

**Paths:** `runtime/src/models/qwen35.cpp`
**Why.** The token loop: embed -> 64 layers (16 attention + 48 GDN) -> final norm -> lm_head -> argmax, with 4 forked streams, events, one decode graph per session (parked per session), adaptive n_splits sized from SM count, and a recursive device_mutex that serializes capture against legacy-stream work. All of it is one-context today.

- [ ] Streams stream/stream_k/stream_v/stream_pf: per-device sets; cross-device event pairs at all-reduce points
- [ ] Decode graph capture (cu_graph + per-session parking lot): capture-on-two-contexts rules; P2P memcpy nodes need peer access ENABLED at capture time (R5)
- [ ] Prefill graph and dflash verify graph: same treatment
- [ ] adaptive n_splits / split tiers: sized for 170 SM (5090); 48 SM cards need retuned constants (kernel subsystem)
- [ ] lm_head: row-split by vocab + cross-device argmax (greedy) / Gumbel-max (temperature)
- [ ] device_mutex semantics with two contexts (the documented capture-poisoning hazard is per-context legacy-stream sync; restate the invariants)
- [ ] Packed decode (decode_packed, graph tiers up to 32 rows): per-device row handling + all-reduce per layer group

### prefill-path — Batched/chunked prefill

**Paths:** `runtime/src/models/qwen35_prefill.cpp`
**Why.** 5.6k-line batched prefill: fused qkvg grouped GEMM, NVFP4/FP8/Int8 GEMM families, chunked prefill, multi-prompt ingestion, DFlash context buffers. Same tensor split as decode, but GEMMs are row-split and their outputs feed the per-device state; the verify (dflash) pass spans both.

- [ ] Fused qkvg operand [2*qdim + 2*kvdim, H]: split rows per q/kv head groups
- [ ] Grouped/batched GEMM split-K variants: partial sums must recombine with the same numerics decision as decode (R2)
- [ ] Chunked prefill windows and GDN state continuation across windows, per device
- [ ] ingest_prompts_packed (multi-session): each session's state on both pools
- [ ] Prefill graph tiers: per-device or hybrid capture

### engine-serving — ContinuousBatchEngine & sessions

**Paths:** `runtime/src/inference_engine.cpp`, `runtime/src/scheduler.cpp`, `runtime/src/prefix_cache.cpp`, `runtime/src/lmcache_bridge_client.cpp`, `runtime/src/lmcache_staging.cpp`
**Why.** One worker thread drives the single device; admission tickets count KV blocks from one pool; prefix-cache entries hold recurrent-state snapshots in pinned HOST memory (device-agnostic); the DSpark drafter and the vision tower are extra models with their own residency; the lmcache sidecar is a FORKED process with its own CUDA setup.

- [ ] Worker: one thread driving both devices (streams, not setDevice) vs one thread per device -- decide (one thread is simpler; streams make it device-independent)
- [ ] Admission: capacity = min over the two KV pools; waiting tickets unchanged
- [ ] open_session/close_session: per-pool block allocation + per-device GDN state
- [ ] DSpark: drafter residency (CHG-0030); verify pass on both devices; the byte-lossless-vs-AR gate must be re-proven inside tp=2 (R2)
- [ ] Prefix cache: snapshots become per-device pairs; LRU policy unchanged (host-resident)
- [ ] Vision tower: run on one device and P2P-broadcast embeddings, or replicate (CHG-0031)
- [ ] lmcache sidecar: its own device assumptions; likely defer the dual-GPU KV tier to v2 (CHG-0017)

### observability — Stats, thermals, metrics

**Paths:** `runtime/src/gpu_stats.cpp`, `runtime/src/thermal_governor.cpp`, `server/src/sparkinfer_server.cpp (/metrics, /v1/info)`
**Why.** query_gpu_stats switches to one device for cudaMemGetInfo (NVML side already resolves by PCI bus id -- device-ordinal independent, good). The thermal governor paces from one card's temperature; /metrics and /v1/info report one GPU.

- [ ] Per-device GpuStats (both cards in /metrics: vram, temp, power, clock)
- [ ] /v1/info: report tp size and per-GPU names (NVIDIA's own docs: 5060 Ti = sm_120, 16 GB, 48 SM)
- [ ] Thermal governor: per-card pacing (consumer cards have low TDP; both may throttle independently) or max-temp policy -- decide
- [ ] device_healthy(): with two cards, one dead context = server down (align with CHG-0034 decision)

### server-config — Server CLI/env surface

**Paths:** `server/src/sparkinfer_server.cpp`, `server/include/model_engine.hpp`, `docker/entrypoint.sh`
**Why.** No way to ask for tp=2: -m/--host/--port/--ctx exist; RuntimeConfig{} hard-codes device 0. New knobs (tp size, explicit device list, per-device budgets) plus docker/entrypoint wiring.

- [ ] --tp N / SPARKINFER_TP (and --devices id,id / SPARKINFER_DEVICES for explicit selection)
- [ ] Validation: N <= device count, requested ids exist, cards are the expected arch (warn otherwise)
- [ ] Pool sizing flags (per-device KV budget) for the server formula
- [ ] Docker: --gpus all already exposes both; entrypoint + gittensor manifest + smoke test with two visible cards
- [ ] Exit-path messages (OOM, no device) rewritten for the two-card world

### tooling-ci — Bench, eval bot, CI, docker

**Paths:** `bench/scripts/`, `eval/`, `.github/workflows/`
**Why.** The whole quality machinery (clock pinning, nvidia-smi head -1, the RTX-5090-pinned eval bot, the rtx5090-required guard) assumes one GPU. tp=2 results need a home in it.

- [ ] _common.sh: clock pinning per GPU (it pins one); detect_arch head -1
- [ ] bench.sh/evaluate.sh/accuracy.sh: run tp=2 on the target box; compare against llama.cpp with --tensor-split 2,2 as the apples-to-apples single-binary baseline
- [ ] Eval bot: new hardware target (dual 5060 Ti box) or a tp=2 arm on existing 5090 pairs; scoring labels (XL..XS) defined for the new target -- governance decision (R6)
- [ ] .github/workflows/rtx5090-required.yml: new guard for tp-related paths (or an explicit exemption)
- [ ] docker image: build unchanged (no new deps), new smoke target

### docs — Documentation

**Paths:** `README.md`, `server/README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/miner-guide.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`
**Why.** Public claims ('full 262,144-token context on one 32 GB card'), the env-var tables, and the miner/PR workflow all describe the single-card world.

- [ ] README: tp=2 on 2x16 GB as a first-class run mode (docker + from-source)
- [ ] server/README: new env/flag rows; /v1/info shape; /metrics additions
- [ ] CONTRIBUTING/miner-guide: how tp=2 PRs are benchmarked (which box, which guards, what counts as regression)
- [ ] CHANGELOG entry on release

### kernels — CUDA kernels (494 __global__, 239 launch_*)

**Paths:** `kernels/csrc/`, `kernels/include/`
**Why.** Most kernels are device-agnostic (pointers in, current context runs them) and need NO code change under tp=2 -- they just run once per device on that device's slice. The audit target is the minority with 5090-shaped assumptions: grid sizes, n_splits tiers, occupancy targets, hardcoded SM counts, and any kernel that reads a tensor larger than one device's slice would hold.

- [ ] Sweep for hardcoded 170/SM-derived constants (n_splits tiers, block counts, chunk sizes)
- [ ] Kernels that address whole tensors (embed, lm_head, qkvg fused operand): confirm they take the per-device slice pointer from the split map, not a full-tensor base+offset that crosses the device boundary
- [ ] flash-decode split counts (adaptive_nsplits_for): retune for 48 SM
- [ ] MoE dispatch kernels: per-device expert ranges
- [ ] No new kernel needed for all-reduce (P2P memcpy + add), but verify the add runs on the right device's stream

### runtime-tests — Unit & integration GPU tests

**Paths:** `runtime/tests/`, `kernels/tests/`
**Why.** ~20 *_gpu_test.cpp files, each cudaSetDevice(0) (or the current device) and one context. The dual-GPU work needs: every existing test green on each device, a P2P copy test, an all-reduce correctness+bandwidth test, and a two-context capture test.

- [ ] Test harness: run the suite with device A, then B (env var), no code change
- [ ] New: p2p_memcpy test, 2-GPU all-reduce (numerics vs host reference), cross-context graph capture test
- [ ] dflash/dspark accuracy tests (bench/scripts/dflash_accuracy.sh) re-pointed at the tp=2 binary
